# Port checklist: Bambu Studio `libslic3r` to Rust

Generated by `python3 scripts/parity/port_checklist.py`; do not edit by hand. Regenerate it in every round
(`--check` fails when the committed copy is stale). The verified entries and their evidence are kept by hand
in `scripts/parity/port_checklist_verified.json`.

C++ side: `references/BambuStudio/src/libslic3r` at the pin (an override-layer file is read in its place).
Rust side: `crates/libslic3r-rs/src`. The parser, the pairing of files and the estimate of how closely a
body follows are those of [`../rust-engine/FILE_MAP.md`](../rust-engine/FILE_MAP.md).

## Overall

**34.7% of the named C++ functions are ported or verified (3144 of 9052); 23.8% by size of body.**

| Status | Functions | Share | By body tokens |
|-|-|-|-|
| not started `[ ]` | 3175 | 35.1% | 47.9% |
| partial `[~]` | 2733 | 30.2% | 28.3% |
| ported `[x]` | 3124 | 34.5% | 23.2% |
| verified `[v]` | 20 | 0.2% | 0.6% |

| Files (C++ units) | Count |
|-|-|
| all | 310 |
| no Rust file of that name | 33 |
| partial | 229 |
| every named function ported | 47 |
| every named function verified | 1 |

C++ still run through the FFI shim crates: 31 functions called from the engine (`clipper-z-sys` 23, `clipper2-z-sys` 5, `eigen-transform-sys` 3, `boost-voronoi-sys` 0). They are listed at
the end; the goal is none.

## How to read it

- A file here is a C++ unit: a `.cpp` with the header of the same name, or a header on its own.
- `[ ]` **not started**: no Rust function of that name anywhere, or the unit has no Rust file.
- `[~]` **partial**: the name exists in Rust but not in the file named after the C++ file, or only another
  overload is ported, or the body is estimated *partial*, *divergent* or a *stub*.
- `[x]` **ported**: in the paired Rust file, body estimated *faithful*. The estimate is mechanical (shared
  identifiers and constants, relative size, no deviation marker); it says where to look, it is not a proof.
  A function the estimate calls partial or divergent counts as ported when a round has read it side by
  side with the C++ and found the same logic; those are listed in `scripts/parity/port_checklist_reviewed.json`
  and say so in their note.
- `[v]` **verified**: ported, and a twin probe agreed with the C++ over a fixture or a test compares with
  C++ output; the evidence is in the note. G-code that matches on three fixtures does not by itself verify
  a function, so this column is short on purpose.
- Constructors, destructors and operators have no name to match by; they are counted per file and are not
  in the percentages. Classes are the scopes the functions sit in; a type with no function body is not listed.
- The percentage counts every named function with a body, accessors included, in all four tiers (tier 4 is
  SLA, which `slicer_cli` does not use).
